LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** **Analyze the spatial contradiction** between evidences and testimonies

From the testimonies, Phoenix Wright states that he gave Doug Swallow a shove, causing him to fall on top of his umbrella. This implies that Doug Swallow was pushed from behind, causing him to fall forward. However, Evidence 2, Crime Photo 1, describes the victim as lying head down, suggesting that he fell backward.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

We can see that Proposition 1 and Proposition 2 are directly related to the direction of the fall based on where Doug Swallow was hit.

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Proposition 1 states that if Doug Swallow is hit from the front, he will fall backward. Proposition 2 states that if Doug Swallow is hit from behind, he will fall forward. Since Phoenix Wright testified that he shoved Doug Swallow from behind, according to Proposition 2, Doug Swallow should have fallen forward. However, the Crime Photo 1 shows Doug Swallow lying head down, indicating a backward fall, which contradicts the expected outcome based on Proposition 2.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

In this case, the listed propositions do apply and help us identify the contradiction.

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** **Analyze the spatial contradiction** between evidences and testimonies

From the testimonies, White describes seeing Mia run to the left and then turn to run for the door. This implies that Mia was moving away from the direction of the attack and towards the door. However, the evidence suggests that Mia was struck with a single blow, which according to the autopsy report (Evidence 5) caused instantaneous death. The spatial contradiction arises from the description of Mia's movement and the nature of the attack as described by White versus the evidence of the murder weapon (Evidence 1) and the location of the stand (Evidence 8).

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

We need to check if any of the spatial propositions can help us understand the contradiction better. Propositions 1 and 2 deal with the direction of the fall based on the direction of the shot, while Proposition 8 deals with the distance of the Thinker from White's office.

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Proposition 8 states: "If the Thinker is inside the stand and the stand is far from White's office then the Thinker is far from White's office." Given that the stand is blocked from view looking from the office (Evidence 8), we can infer that the stand is indeed far from White's office. Therefore, the Thinker (the murder weapon) would also be far from White's office.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

Since Proposition 8 applies, we can use it to reason that if the Thinker (the murder weapon) is far from White's office, it would be difficult or impossible for him to see the exact sequence of events as he described. Specifically, White's testimony (Testimony 3) claims that he saw the killer attack Mia and that she dodged, which contradicts the idea that he could not clearly see the weapon or the attacker due to the distance and obstruction.

None of the listed propositions directly address the contradiction. The contradiction arises from the spatial inconsistency of where the body was hidden. However, Evidence 7 suggests that the Taurusaurus Head fell from the roof of the trailer, indicating that the body was initially on the roof, which is unlikely to have dirt inside the costume.

However, Evidence 11 states that Ms. Hart was in the 51st-floor storeroom the night before last, but did not see the president pass through. This suggests that the 51st floor was accessible from the roof of the Bigg Building, and Ms. Hart would have seen anyone entering or leaving the 51st floor via the hatch.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

We need to check if any of the spatial propositions provided can help us identify a contradiction based on the spatial information given.

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Propositions 1, 3, and 4 are relevant here:
- Prop 1 states that if Oliver Deacon is shot from above, the bullet comes from the upper direction.
- Prop 3 states that if Oliver Deacon is shot upward, the trajectory goes from low to high.
- Prop 4 states that if Oliver Deacon is shot downward, the trajectory goes from high to low.

Given the bullet's entry and exit points, the trajectory is from high to low, which aligns with Prop 4. However, the testimonies suggest that Oliver Deacon was shot from above, which would imply an upward trajectory according to Prop 1, but this contradicts the evidence showing a downward trajectory.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

In this case, the propositions do apply, and they help us identify the contradiction. **Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial propositions from the list above apply to this contradiction

We need to check if any of the spatial propositions can help us understand the contradiction better. Propositions related to the Skulkin Brothers and the location of the gunshot might be relevant.

**Step 3:** If applicable propositions exist, explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Proposition 9 states: "If the Skulkin Brothers are inside the pawnbrokery and the pawnbrokery is behind the storeroom then the Skulkin Brothers are behind the storeroom."

This proposition does not directly address the contradiction but helps establish the relative position of the Skulkin Brothers and the storeroom. However, Proposition 12 states: "If the gunshot is heard from the storeroom and the storeroom is behind the main shop then the gunshot originates from behind the main shop."

Given that the gunshot was heard from the storeroom and the storeroom is behind the main shop, Proposition 12 implies that the gunshot originated from behind the main shop, which aligns with the location of the victim, Mr Windibank, in the storeroom.

**Step 4:** If none of the listed propositions apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

The contradiction arises because the Skulkin brothers claim they were inside the pawnbrokery when the gunshot was heard, yet the Crime Scene Photograph places the victim in the storeroom, suggesting the gunshot originated from within the storeroom. Since the Skulkin brothers were supposedly inside the pawnbrokery, they should have been aware of the exact location of the gunshot, but their testimonies do not specify this detail, and they claim they were not involved in the shooting.
